(yes/deferred/n-a) | How / Why-deferred |\n|---|---------|:------------------------------:|---|\n| 1 | Prompt Chaining | deferred | O grafo \u00c9 a cadeia (output de n\u00f3 \u2192 input do filho via portas tipadas), mas o executor \u00e9 Fase 1 \u2192 `FR-SPACES-005`. Primitivas vivas: `getTopologicalLayers` (`src/lib/canvas-dag.ts`, testada) + `port-compat.ts` promovido. |\n| 2 | Routing | deferred | `modelKey='auto'` = roteamento server-side por custo/qualidade (`UI-SPACES-010`, espelha `calc_agent_cost_mcoin`) \u2014 Fase 1, junto do executor. |\n| 3 | Parallelization | deferred | Execu\u00e7\u00e3o camada-a-camada com n\u00f3s da mesma layer em paralelo \u2192 `FR-SPACES-005` (Fase 1). |\n| 4 | Reflection | n-a | Sem la\u00e7o Generator-Critic no m\u00f3dulo; a reflex\u00e3o visual (Vision-QA gate) vive no harness de QA do ecossistema (`audit-canvas-ui.ts`), n\u00e3o no produto. |\n| 5 | Tool Use | yes | Execu\u00e7\u00e3o de n\u00f3 = invoca\u00e7\u00e3o de edge function tipada; `canvas-execute` (com `CREDIT_COSTS`, `index.ts:15`) **j\u00e1 vive em produ\u00e7\u00e3o** servindo o Canvas Studio \u2014 Spaces o reusa por contrato `BR-SPACES-001` (zero tool novo, anti-drift). |\n| 6 | Planning | n-a | O \"plano\" (DAG) \u00e9 autorado pelo **humano** no canvas; n\u00e3o h\u00e1 gera\u00e7\u00e3o ag\u00eantica de plano neste m\u00f3dulo. |\n| 7 | Multi-Agent | n-a | Superf\u00edcie single-user; Workflow App encapsula **subgrafo** (`PR-SPACES-007`), n\u00e3o coletivo de agentes. |\n| 8 | Memory Management | yes | Malha: seal `documentation_suite` desta su\u00edte + observation node por 1\u00ba run (`PROC-SPACES-008`, Mesh Connection Mandate). Sess\u00e3o: time-travel `zundo` (limit 100) promovido (`src/store/useSpacesStore.ts:158-162`). |\n| 9 | Learning & Adaptation | deferred | Clusters de gera\u00e7\u00e3o (`FR-SPACES-012`) s\u00e3o o futuro sinal de reward, mas o loop fechado \u00e9 d\u00edvida de ecossistema \u2192 `OTD-AG-002` (doutrina \u00a73). |\n| 10 | Model Context Protocol | n-a | N\u00e3o exp\u00f5e nem consome MCP em runtime (Vision MCP \u00e9 m\u00f3dulo irm\u00e3o). |\n| 11 | Goal Setting & Monitoring | deferred | Metas e KPIs definidos (`QO/KPI-SPACES-###` em `08-quality-metrics.md`); a emiss\u00e3o de telemetria (`infra_health_logs` events `spaces_*`) nasce na Fase 1. |\n| 12 | Exception Handling & Recovery | deferred | Especificado por inteiro \u2014 refund-on-failure `BR-SPACES-004` (espelha `finalize_video_render` vivo), descendentes `blocked`, rollback otimista `FR-SPACES-008`, draft recovery `FR-SPACES-011` \u2014 implementa\u00e7\u00e3o Fase 1. |\n| 13 | Human-in-the-Loop | yes | Custo vis\u00edvel **antes** do d\u00e9bito \u00e9 contrato do produto (`MR-SPACES-006`/`BR-SPACES-002`) com artefato promovido: `estimateCost`/`projectGraphCost` (`src/hooks/useParamRegistry.ts:75-84`). Dry-run sem d\u00e9bito (`BR-SPACES-005`); todo Run \u00e9 decis\u00e3o humana. |\n| 14 | Knowledge Retrieval (RAG) | n-a | O canvas n\u00e3o recupera da malha em runtime (MVP); refer\u00eancia cross-space \u00e9 por FK de asset (`FR-SPACES-009`), n\u00e3o sem\u00e2ntica. |\n| 15 | Inter-Agent Communication (A2A) | n-a | Sem troca de mensagens entre agentes. |\n| 16 | Resource-Aware Optimization | yes | Ledger-first \u00e9 a regra-m\u00e3e da BRD (estimar\u2192reservar\u2192debitar\u2192reconciliar, `BR \u00a75`); autoridade de pre\u00e7o server-side viva (`CREDIT_COSTS`); espelho client display-only promovido (`CLIENT_COST_MIRROR`, `useParamRegistry.ts:30`) com gate anti-drift (`FMEA-SPACES-004`/`SOL-SPACES-001`). |\n| 17 | Reasoning Techniques | n-a | Sem racioc\u00ednio LLM pr\u00f3prio; o n\u00f3 Assistant delega ao `aios-chat`/`generate-content` (m\u00f3dulos existentes). |\n| 18 | Guardrails / Safety | yes | Gates promovidos e vivos no c\u00f3digo: `canConnect` (`port-compat.ts` \u2014 \u00fanica fonte de verdade de conex\u00e3o), `SpaceNodeDataSchema.parse` no `addNode` (dado inv\u00e1lido nunca entra no store), guard `locked`. Planejados na Fase 1: RLS default-deny 404-n\u00e3o-403 (`FR-SPACES-007`) + guards de prompt pr\u00e9-d\u00e9bito j\u00e1 vivos no `canvas-execute` (`BR \u00a74`). |\n| 19 | Evaluation & Monitoring | deferred | FMEA + KPIs selados (`08-quality-metrics.md`) e gate E2E 1920\u00d71080/Vision-QA j\u00e1 mandat\u00f3rio para Canvas; instrumenta\u00e7\u00e3o do m\u00f3dulo (eventos `spaces_*`) \u00e9 Fase 1 \u2192 `KPI-SPACES-004` como invariante-m\u00e3e. |\n| 20 | Prioritization | n-a | Sem scheduler/\u00e1lgebra de prioriza\u00e7\u00e3o pr\u00f3pria; a fila respeita limite do plano (`PR-SPACES-009`) \u2014 pol\u00edtica de recurso, n\u00e3o prioriza\u00e7\u00e3o ag\u00eantica (gap de ecossistema \u2192 OTD-AG-020, n\u00e3o deste m\u00f3dulo). |\n| 21 | Exploration & Discovery | deferred | Branching + clusters lado-a-lado (`MR-SPACES-004`/`PR-SPACES-010`) = explora\u00e7\u00e3o **humana assistida** pelo canvas; torneio generativo \u00e9 d\u00edvida de ecossistema \u2192 `OTD-AG-008`. |\n\n**Placar:** `yes` \u00d7 4 (#5 Tool Use \u00b7 #8 Memory \u00b7 #13 HITL \u00b7 #16 Resource-Aware) \u00b7 `deferred` \u00d7 8 (#1, #2, #3, #9, #11, #12, #19, #21 \u2014 todos com FR/OTD nomeado) \u00b7 `n-a` \u00d7 9. A concentra\u00e7\u00e3o de `yes` em **Tool Use + Memory + HITL + Resource-Aware** reflete a natureza declarada: uma superf\u00edcie de produto ledger-gated com humano no comando, cuja camada de execu\u00e7\u00e3o chega na Fase 1 \u2014 quando os 8 `deferred` devem flipar (gate de re-declara\u00e7\u00e3o no seal da Fase 1).\n\n---\n\n*Fim da su\u00edte mandatada (01\u201308 + addenda 09\u201312). \u00cdndice e mesh em `00-index.md`.*"
}

} else {\n    console.log(`\ud83d\udd17 DERIVES_FROM edge: ${edge.id} \u2192 ${PARENT_SEAL} (${parent.id})`);\n  }\n} else {\n  console.log(`\u26a0\ufe0f  Parent seal ${PARENT_SEAL} not found \u2014 edge skipped.`);\n}\n\nconsole.log(`\\n\ud83e\udde0 Embedding fires automatically via trg_mcorch_embed_on_insert (pg_net async).`);\nconsole.log(`   Confirm: SELECT embedding IS NOT NULL FROM mcorch_nodes WHERE id = '${node.id}';`);\n"
}
```

# /loop — schedule a recurring or self-paced prompt

Parse the input below into `[interval] <prompt…>` and schedule it.

## Parsing (in priority order)

1. **Leading token**: if the first whitespace-delimited token matches `^\d+[smhd]$` (e.g. `5m`, `2h`), that's the interval; the rest is the prompt.
2. **Trailing "every" clause**: otherwise, if the input ends with `every <N><unit>` or `every <N> <unit-word>` (e.g. `every 20m`, `every 5 minutes`, `every 2 hours`), extract that as the interval and strip it from the prompt. Only match when what follows "every" is a time expression — `check every PR` has no interval.
3. **No interval**: otherwise, the entire input is the prompt and you'll self-pace dynamically (see "Dynamic mode" below).

If the resulting prompt is empty, show usage `/loop [interval] <prompt>` and stop.

Examples:
- `5m /babysit-prs` → interval `5m`, prompt `/babysit-prs` (rule 1)
- `check the deploy every 20m` → interval `20m`, prompt `check the deploy` (rule 2)
- `run tests every 5 minutes` → interval `5m`, prompt `run tests` (rule 2)
- `check the deploy` → no interval → dynamic mode, prompt `check the deploy` (rule 3)
- `check every PR` → no interval → dynamic mode, prompt `check every PR` (rule 3 — "every" not followed by time)
- `5m` → empty prompt → show usage

## Offer cloud first

Before any scheduling step, check whether EITHER is true:
- the parsed interval (rule 1 or 2) is **≥60 minutes**, or
- regardless of which rule matched, the original input uses daily phrasing ("every morning", "daily", "every day", "each night", "every weekday")

If either is true, call AskUserQuestion first:
- `question`: "This loop stops when you close this session. Set it up as a cloud schedule instead so it keeps running?"
- `header`: "Schedule"
- `options`: `[{label: "Cloud schedule (recommended)", description: "Runs in Anthropic's cloud even after you close this session"}, {label: "This session only", description: "Runs in this terminal until you exit"}]`

If they pick **Cloud schedule**: do NOT call CronCreate. Invoke the `schedule` skill directly via the Skill tool with `args` set to their original input verbatim (e.g. `Skill({skill: "schedule", args: "every morning tell me a joke"})`), then follow that skill's instructions to completion. Do NOT tell the user to run /schedule themselves. **Then stop — do not continue to any section below** (no CronCreate, no ScheduleWakeup, no "execute the prompt now").
If they pick **This session only**:
- If the trigger was a parsed ≥60-minute interval (rule 1 or 2): continue below with that interval.
- If the trigger was daily phrasing only (rule 3, no parsed interval): do NOT call CronCreate. Explain that a daily-cadence loop won't fire before this session closes, so there's nothing useful to schedule locally — suggest they either pick Cloud schedule, or re-run `/loop` with an explicit shorter interval (e.g. `/loop 1h <prompt>`) if they want a session loop. Then stop.
If neither trigger condition was met: continue below.

## Fixed-interval mode (rules 1 and 2)

Convert the interval to a cron expression:

| Interval pattern      | Cron expression     | Notes                                    |
|-----------------------|---------------------|------------------------------------------|
| `Nm` where N ≤ 59   | `*/N * * * *`     | every N minutes                          |
| `Nm` where N ≥ 60   | `0 */H * * *`     | round to hours (H = N/60, must divide 24)|
| `Nh` where N ≤ 23   | `0 */N * * *`     | every N hours                            |
| `Nd`                | `0 0 */N * *`     | every N days at midnight local           |
| `Ns`                | treat as `ceil(N/60)m` | cron minimum granularity is 1 minute  |

**If the interval doesn't cleanly divide its unit** (e.g. `7m` → `*/7 * * * *` gives uneven gaps at :56→:00; `90m` → 1.5h which cron can't express), pick the nearest clean interval and tell the user what you rounded to before scheduling.

Then:
1. Call CronCreate with: `cron` (the expression above), `prompt` (the parsed prompt verbatim), `recurring: true`.
2. Briefly confirm: what's scheduled, the cron expression, the human-readable cadence, that recurring tasks auto-expire after 7 days, and that the user can cancel sooner with CronDelete (include the job ID). Only if you did NOT show the cloud-offer AskUserQuestion above (i.e., neither trigger condition applied), end the confirmation with this exact line on its own, italicized: `_Runs until you close this session · For durable cloud-based loops, use /schedule_`. If the user already answered that question, omit this line.
3. **Then immediately execute the parsed prompt now** — don't wait for the first cron fire. If it's a slash command, invoke it via the Skill tool; otherwise act on it directly.

## Dynamic mode (rule 3 — no interval)

The user wants you to self-pace. Decide what makes the next iteration worth running — a passage of time, or an observable event.

1. **Run the parsed prompt now.** If it's a slash command, invoke it via the Skill tool; otherwise act on it directly.
2. **If the next run is gated on an event** (CI finishing, a log line matching, a file changing, a PR comment) and no Monitor is already running for it: arm one now with `persistent: true`. Its events arrive as `<task-notification>` messages and wake this loop immediately — you do not wait for the ScheduleWakeup deadline. Arm once; on later iterations call TaskList first and skip this step if a monitor is already running.
3. **Briefly confirm**: that you're self-pacing, whether a Monitor is the primary wake signal, that you ran the task now, and what fallback delay you're about to pick. Write this as text *before* calling ScheduleWakeup — the turn ends as soon as that tool returns.
4. **Then, as the last action of this turn, call ScheduleWakeup** with:
   - `delaySeconds`: with a Monitor armed this is the **fallback heartbeat** — how long to wait if no event fires (lean 1200–1800s; idle ticks past the 5-minute cache window are pure overhead). Without a Monitor this is the cadence — pick based on what you observed. Read the tool's own description for cache-aware delay guidance.
   - `reason`: one short sentence on why you picked that delay.
   - `prompt`: the full original /loop input verbatim, prefixed with `/loop ` so the next firing re-enters this skill and continues the loop. For example, if the user typed `/loop check the deploy`, pass `/loop check the deploy` as the prompt.
5. **If you were woken by a `<task-notification>`** rather than this prompt: handle the event in the context of the loop task, then call ScheduleWakeup again with the same `prompt` and the same 1200–1800s `delaySeconds` from step 4 — the Monitor remains the wake signal; this only resets the safety net.
6. **To stop the loop**, omit the ScheduleWakeup call and TaskStop any Monitor you armed (use TaskList to find the task ID if it is no longer in context). Before you stop, send a one-line outcome via PushNotification — the user may be away and waiting to hear it's done. Skip this if you're stopping because the user just told you to; they're already here.

> ```
> 1	// useAutopilot — Viral Autopilot data harness (FR-VA-014). All server state via TanStack Query (Harness Engineering).
> 2	// CRUD of autopilot_plans (RLS own), cycle history, "run now" (invokes autopilot-run on the user-JWT path), and
> 3	// activate/pause. The cadence cron / paid execution live server-side; this hook is the display-only data layer.
> 4	import { useQuery, useMutation, useQueryClient } from '@tanstack/react-query';
> 5	import { supabase } from '@/integrations/supabase/client';
> 6	import { useAuth } from '@/hooks/useAuth';
> 7	import { edgeErrorMessage } from '@/lib/edge';
> 8	import type { Tables, TablesInsert } from '@/integrations/supabase/types';
> 9	
> 10	export type AutopilotPlan = Tables<'autopilot_plans'>;
> 11	export type AutopilotCycle = Tables<'autopilot_cycles'>;
> 12	
> 13	export const AUTOPILOT_PLATFORMS = [
> 14	  { id: 'wordpress', label: 'WordPress' },
> 15	  { id: 'linkedin', label: 'LinkedIn' },
> 16	  { id: 'twitter', label: 'X / Twitter' },
> 17	] as const;
> 18	
> 19	export interface AutopilotPlanInput {
> 20	  name: string;
> 21	  mode: 'all_at_once' | 'every_n_days';
> 22	  interval_days: number;
> 23	  target_product_ids: string[];
> 24	  platforms: string[];
> 25	  budget_cap_mco: number;
> 26	  daily_cap_mco: number;
> 27	  min_balance_mco: number;
> 28	  ab_variants: number;
> 29	}
> 30	
> 31	export function useAutopilotPlans() {
> 32	  const { user } = useAuth();
> 33	  return useQuery({
> 34	    queryKey: ['autopilot-plans'],
> 35	    enabled: !!user,
> 36	    queryFn: async (): Promise<AutopilotPlan[]> => {
> 37	      const { data, error } = await supabase
> 38	        .from('autopilot_plans').select('*').order('created_at', { ascending: false });
> 39	      if (error) throw error;
> 40	      return (data ?? []) as AutopilotPlan[];
> 41	    },
> 42	  });
> 43	}
> 44	
> 45	export function useAutopilotCycles(planId?: string) {
> 46	  return useQuery({
> 47	    queryKey: ['autopilot-cycles', planId],
> 48	    enabled: !!planId,
> 49	    queryFn: async (): Promise<AutopilotCycle[]> => {
> 50	      const { data, error } = await supabase
> 51	        .from('autopilot_cycles').select('*').eq('plan_id', planId!)
> 52	        .order('cycle_number', { ascending: false }).limit(20);
> 53	      if (error) throw error;
> 54	      return (data ?? []) as AutopilotCycle[];
> 55	    },
> 56	  });
> 57	}
> 58	
> 59	export function useCreateAutopilotPlan() {
> 60	  const qc = useQueryClient();
> 61	  const { user } = useAuth();
> 62	  return useMutation({
> 63	    mutationFn: async (input: AutopilotPlanInput): Promise<string> => {
> 64	      if (!user) throw new Error('Sessão expirada — faça login novamente.');
> 65	      const row: TablesInsert<'autopilot_plans'> = {
> 66	        user_id: user.id,
> 67	        name: input.name,
> 68	        mode: input.mode,
> 69	        interval_days: input.mode === 'every_n_days' ? input.interval_days : null,
> 70	        target_product_ids: input.target_product_ids,
> 71	        platforms: input.platforms,
> 72	        budget_cap_mco: input.budget_cap_mco,
> 73	        daily_cap_mco: input.daily_cap_mco,
> 74	        min_balance_mco: input.min_balance_mco,
> 75	        ab_variants: input.ab_variants,
> 76	        is_active: false,
> 77	      };
> 78	      const { data, error } = await supabase.from('autopilot_plans').insert(row).select('id').single();
> 79	      if (error) throw error;
> 80	      return data.id;
> 81	    },
> 82	    onSuccess: () => qc.invalidateQueries({ queryKey: ['autopilot-plans'] }),
> 83	  });
> 84	}
> 85	
> 86	export function useSetPlanActive() {
> 87	  const qc = useQueryClient();
> 88	  return useMutation({
> 89	    mutationFn: async ({ id, active }: { id: string; active: boolean }) => {
> 90	      const patch: Record<string, unknown> = { is_active: active };
> 91	      // Activate → arm next_run_at to now so the cadence cron picks the plan up on its next tick.
> 92	      if (active) patch.next_run_at = new Date().toISOString();
> 93	      const { error } = await supabase.from('autopilot_plans').update(patch).eq('id', id);
> 94	      if (error) throw error;
> 95	    },
> 96	    onSuccess: () => qc.invalidateQueries({ queryKey: ['autopilot-plans'] }),
> 97	  });
> 98	}
> 99	
> 100	export interface RunResult {
> 101	  ok?: boolean; dry_run?: boolean; n_runs?: number; projected_mco?: number;
> 102	  cycle_id?: string; succeeded?: number; actual_mco?: number; refunded_mco?: number; reason?: string;
> 103	}
> 104	
> 105	export function useRunAutopilot() {
> 106	  const qc = useQueryClient();
> 107	  return useMutation({
> 108	    mutationFn: async ({ planId, dryRun }: { planId: string; dryRun?: boolean }): Promise<RunResult> => {
> 109	      const { data, error } = await supabase.functions.invoke('autopilot-run', {
> 110	        body: { plan_id: planId, dry_run: !!dryRun },
> 111	      });
> 112	      if (error) throw new Error(await edgeErrorMessage(error, 'Falha ao executar o Autopilot'));
> 113	      return data as RunResult;
> 114	    },
> 115	    onSuccess: (_d, vars) => {
> 116	      qc.invalidateQueries({ queryKey: ['autopilot-plans'] });
> 117	      qc.invalidateQueries({ queryKey: ['autopilot-cycles', vars.planId] });
> 118	    },
> 119	  });
> 120	}
> 121	
> ```

> 152	function CycleList({ planId }: { planId: string }) {
> 153	  const { data: cycles = [], isLoading } = useAutopilotCycles(planId);
> 154	  if (isLoading) return <div className="text-sm text-muted-foreground py-2">Carregando ciclos…</div>;
> 155	  if (cycles.length === 0) return <div className="text-sm text-muted-foreground py-2">Nenhum ciclo ainda.</div>;
> 156	  return (
> 157	    <div className="space-y-1.5 pt-2">
> 158	      {cycles.map((c) => (
> 159	        <div key={c.id} className="flex items-center justify-between text-sm rounded-md bg-muted/30 px-3 py-1.5">
> 160	          <span className="font-mono text-xs">#{c.cycle_number}</span>
> 161	          <Badge variant="secondary" className={CYCLE_BADGE[c.status] ?? ''}>{c.status}</Badge>
> 162	          <span className="text-muted-foreground">{c.planned_units} criativos</span>
> 163	          <span className="text-muted-foreground">−{c.spend_mco} / +{c.refunded_mco} mco</span>
> 164	          <span className="text-xs text-muted-foreground">{c.completed_at ? new Date(c.completed_at).toLocaleDateString('pt-BR') : '—'}</span>
> 165	        </div>
> 166	      ))}
> 167	    </div>
> 168	  );
> 169	}
> 170	
> 171	function PlanCard({ plan }: { plan: AutopilotPlan }) {
> 172	  const setActive = useSetPlanActive();
> 173	  const run = useRunAutopilot();
> 174	  const [showCycles, setShowCycles] = useState(false);
> 175	
> 176	  const doRun = async (dryRun: boolean) => {
> 177	    try {
> 178	      const r = await run.mutateAsync({ planId: plan.id, dryRun });
> 179	      if (dryRun) {
> 180	        toast({ title: 'Simulação (sem gasto)', description: `${r.n_runs} criativos · ${r.projected_mco} mcoCoins projetados.` });
> 181	      } else if (r.ok) {
> 182	        toast({ title: 'Ciclo executado', description: `${r.succeeded}/${r.n_runs} ok · ${r.actual_mco} gastos · ${r.refunded_mco} estornados.` });
> 183	      } else {
> 184	        toast({ title: 'Ciclo abortado', description: REASON_LABEL[r.reason ?? ''] ?? r.reason, variant: 'destructive' });
> 185	      }
> 186	    } catch (e) {
> 187	      toast({ title: 'Falha ao executar', description: (e as Error).message, variant: 'destructive' });
> 188	    }
> 189	  };
> 190	
> 191	  const togglePause = async () => {
> 192	    try {
> 193	      await setActive.mutateAsync({ id: plan.id, active: !plan.is_active });
> 194	      toast({ title: plan.is_active ? 'Plano pausado' : 'Plano ativado', description: plan.is_active ? '' : 'A cadência o executará no próximo ciclo do cron.' });
> 195	    } catch (e) {
> 196	      toast({ title: 'Falha', description: (e as Error).message, variant: 'destructive' });
> 197	    }
> 198	  };
> 199	
> 200	  return (
> 201	    <Card>
> 202	      <CardHeader className="pb-3">
> 203	        <div className="flex items-start justify-between gap-3">
> 204	          <CardTitle className="text-base flex items-center gap-2">
> 205	            <Rocket className="h-4 w-4 text-primary" />{plan.name}
> 206	          </CardTitle>
> 207	          <Badge className={plan.is_active ? 'bg-emerald-500/15 text-emerald-400' : 'bg-muted text-muted-foreground'}>
> 208	            {plan.is_active ? 'Ativo' : 'Pausado'}
> 209	          </Badge>
> 210	        </div>
> 211	      </CardHeader>
> 212	      <CardContent className="space-y-3">
> 213	        <div className="flex flex-wrap gap-1.5">
> 214	          {(plan.platforms ?? []).map((p) => <Badge key={p} variant="outline">{p}</Badge>)}
> 215	          <Badge variant="outline">{(plan.target_product_ids ?? []).length} produtos</Badge>
> 216	          <Badge variant="outline">{plan.mode === 'every_n_days' ? `a cada ${plan.interval_days}d` : 'uma vez'}</Badge>
> 217	        </div>
> 218	        <div className="text-xs text-muted-foreground">
> 219	          Tetos: {plan.budget_cap_mco} mco/ciclo · {plan.daily_cap_mco} mco/dia · piso {plan.min_balance_mco} · A/B {plan.ab_variants}
> 220	          {plan.next_run_at && <> · próximo: {new Date(plan.next_run_at).toLocaleString('pt-BR')}</>}
> 221	        </div>
> 222	        <div className="flex flex-wrap gap-2">
> 223	          <Button size="sm" variant="secondary" onClick={() => doRun(true)} disabled={run.isPending}>
> 224	            <FlaskConical className="mr-1.5 h-3.5 w-3.5" />Simular
> 225	          </Button>
> 226	          <Button size="sm" onClick={() => doRun(false)} disabled={run.isPending}>
> 227	            {run.isPending ? <Loader2 className="mr-1.5 h-3.5 w-3.5 animate-spin" /> : <Play className="mr-1.5 h-3.5 w-3.5" />}Rodar agora
> 228	          </Button>
> 229	          <Button size="sm" variant="outline" onClick={togglePause} disabled={setActive.isPending}>
> 230	            {plan.is_active ? <><Pause className="mr-1.5 h-3.5 w-3.5" />Pausar</> : <><Play className="mr-1.5 h-3.5 w-3.5" />Ativar</>}
> 231	          </Button>
> 232	          <Button size="sm" variant="ghost" onClick={() => setShowCycles((s) => !s)}>
> 233	            <History className="mr-1.5 h-3.5 w-3.5" />{showCycles ? 'Ocultar' : 'Ciclos'}
> 234	          </Button>
> 235	        </div>
> 236	        {showCycles && <CycleList planId={plan.id} />}
> 237	      </CardContent>
> 238	    </Card>
> 239	  );
> 240	}
> 241	
> 242	export default function AutopilotPage() {
> 243	  const { data: plans = [], isLoading } = useAutopilotPlans();
> 244	  return (
> 245	    <div className="p-6 space-y-6 max-w-4xl mx-auto">
> 246	      <header className="flex items-center justify-between gap-4">
> 247	        <div>
> 248	          <h1 className="text-2xl font-bold flex items-center gap-2"><Rocket className="h-6 w-6 text-primary" />Viral Autopilot</h1>
> 249	          <p className="text-sm text-muted-foreground">Conteúdo viral monetizado, gerado e agendado em piloto automático — que melhora a cada ciclo.</p>
> 250	        </div>
> 251	        <CreatePlanDialog />
> 252	      </header>
> 253	
> 254	      {isLoading ? (
> 255	        <div className="flex items-center justify-center py-16 text-muted-foreground"><Loader2 className="h-6 w-6 animate-spin" /></div>
> 256	      ) : plans.length === 0 ? (
> 257	        <Card><CardContent className="py-12 text-center space-y-2">
> 258	          <Rocket className="h-8 w-8 mx-auto text-muted-foreground" />
> 259	          <p className="text-muted-foreground">Nenhum plano ainda. Crie um para a constelação começar a produzir sozinha.</p>
> 260	        </CardContent></Card>
> 261	      ) : (
> 262	        <div className="grid gap-4">{plans.map((p) => <PlanCard key={p.id} plan={p} />)}</div>
> 263	      )}
> 264	    </div>
> 265	  );
> 266	}
> 267	
> ```

  "content": "# SOP \u2014 Pauta de Oportunidades de Receita (FR-VA-031 \u00b7 HITL)\n\n> **Lei 2 (Processo Antecipado).** SOP do processo humano que a superf\u00edcie `RevenuePauta`\n> (AutopilotPage) automatiza a *apresenta\u00e7\u00e3o* \u2014 a **decis\u00e3o permanece humana** por design\n> (\"Receita = pauta humana, n\u00e3o otimiza\u00e7\u00e3o cega\", `docs/bok/viral-autopilot/04-frd.md` FR-VA-031).\n> Criada 2026-07-02, antes do c\u00f3digo (loop aut\u00f4nomo).\n\n## Operator\n\n**Sovereign (Gabriel)** \u2014 \u00fanico decisor de monetiza\u00e7\u00e3o. A UI apenas ranqueia e apresenta;\nnenhuma a\u00e7\u00e3o de monetiza\u00e7\u00e3o \u00e9 executada automaticamente.\n\n## Sequence (como o operator decide HOJE, manualmente)\n\n1. Abrir `/dashboard/autopilot` \u2192 se\u00e7\u00e3o **\"Pauta de Receita\"**.\n2. Ler os candidatos ranqueados \u2014 cada linha \u00e9 um criativo com desempenho REAL coletado\n   (`creative_metrics`: impress\u00f5es, engajamentos, hook rate, cliques de afiliado) e o\n   status de monetiza\u00e7\u00e3o derivado de `affiliate_links.content_id`:\n   - **candidato** \u2014 engajamento alto e NENHUM link de afiliado anexado (a oportunidade);\n   - **monetizado** \u2014 tem link, ainda sem convers\u00e3o (`revenue_cents = 0`);\n   - **convertendo** \u2014 tem link E receita real (`revenue_cents > 0`).\n3. Avaliar **retorno \u00d7 esfor\u00e7o \u00d7 prioridade** (colunas da UI):\n   - *Retorno* = sinal de engajamento real (taxa + volume) \u2014 nunca proje\u00e7\u00e3o fabricada;\n   - *Esfor\u00e7o* = \"Baixo \u2014 anexar link ML\" quando o criativo j\u00e1 tem `product_id` rastreado;\n     \"M\u00e9dio \u2014 definir produto\" quando n\u00e3o tem;\n   - *Prioridade* = posi\u00e7\u00e3o no rank (candidatos primeiro, por engajamento).\n4. Decidir por criativo: monetizar (anexar link ML via fluxo vigente `process-affiliate-link`\n   / painel de afiliados) \u00b7 re-amplificar (nova varia\u00e7\u00e3o no plano) \u00b7 ignorar.\n\n## Verification gates\n\n| Gate | Crit\u00e9rio material |\n|---|---|\n| G1 \u2014 dado real | Toda linha da pauta corresponde a rows reais de `creative_metrics` do tenant (RLS own); lista vazia mostra empty-state honesto, nunca placeholder fabricado. |\n| G2 \u2014 status correto | `monetizado`/`convertendo` batem com `affiliate_links` (join client-side por `content_id`); um criativo com link nunca aparece como `candidato`. |\n| G3 \u2014 zero side-effect | A pauta \u00e9 read-only: nenhuma muta\u00e7\u00e3o/d\u00e9bito ao renderizar ou ranquear. |\n| G4 \u2014 ranking puro testado | `rankRevenueCandidates()` \u00e9 fun\u00e7\u00e3o pura com testes vitest (agrega\u00e7\u00e3o, ordena\u00e7\u00e3o candidato-primeiro, guard divis\u00e3o-por-zero). |\n\n## Recovery path\n\n- Query falhou \u2192 card mostra erro pt-BR + bot\u00e3o de recarregar (TanStack `refetch`); nunca\n  lista parcial silenciosa.\n- M\u00e9tricas ausentes (coletor FR-VA-028 ainda n\u00e3o rodou) \u2192 empty-state \"coletando desempenho\"\n  (espelha o insight card do ciclo 1) \u2014 **n\u00e3o** degradar para dados de outra fonte.\n\n## Success signal\n\nO Sovereign abre a pauta e v\u00ea criativos reais ranqueados com status de monetiza\u00e7\u00e3o correto\n(provado por browser-verify + confer\u00eancia de 1 linha contra o DB). A decis\u00e3o de monetizar\ncontinua sendo dele \u2014 a superf\u00edcie s\u00f3 elimina a garimpagem manual de m\u00e9tricas.\n"
}

{
  "file_path": "/home/gcrUX/htdocs/constellation-orchestra/src/lib/revenue-pauta.ts",
  "content": "// revenue-pauta.ts \u2014 pure ranking logic for the Revenue Opportunity Pauta (FR-VA-031, HITL).\n// Aggregates real collected performance (creative_metrics) and derives monetization status from\n// affiliate_links. Pure + display-only math: no fabricated projections, no side effects \u2014 the\n// monetization DECISION stays human (SOP docs/processes/revenue-opportunity-pauta.md).\n\nexport interface CreativeMetricRow {\n  content_variant_id: string;\n  impressions: number;\n  engagements: number;\n  hook_rate: number | null;\n  affiliate_clicks: number;\n  revenue_cents: number;\n  platform: string | null;\n  product_id: string | null;\n  collected_at: string;\n}\n\nexport interface AffiliateLinkRow {\n  content_id: string | null;\n  revenue_cents: number;\n  clicks: number;\n  conversions: number;\n}\n\n/** candidato = high-signal creative with NO affiliate link yet (the opportunity) \u00b7\n *  monetizado = link attached, no revenue yet \u00b7 convertendo = real revenue attributed. */\nexport type PautaStatus = 'candidato' | 'monetizado' | 'convertendo';\n\nexport interface PautaEntry {\n  contentId: string;\n  impressions: number;\n  engagements: number;\n  /** engagements / impressions (0 when impressions = 0 \u2014 never NaN) */\n  engagementRate: number;\n  hookRate: number | null;\n  affiliateClicks: number;\n  revenueCents: number;\n  platforms: string[];\n  productId: string | null;\n  status: PautaStatus;\n  /** low = product already tracked (attach ML link) \u00b7 medium = product still undefined */\n  effort: 'low' | 'medium';\n  lastCollectedAt: string;\n}\n\nconst STATUS_ORDER: Record<PautaStatus, number> = { candidato: 0, monetizado: 1, convertendo: 2 };\n\nexport function rankRevenueCandidates(\n  metrics: CreativeMetricRow[],\n  links: AffiliateLinkRow[],\n): PautaEntry[] {\n  const linksByContent = new Map<string, { revenue: number; clicks: number; conversions: number }>();\n  for (const l of links) {\n    if (!l.content_id) continue;\n    const acc = linksByContent.get(l.content_id) ?? { revenue: 0, clicks: 0, conversions: 0 };\n    acc.revenue += l.revenue_cents ?? 0;\n    acc.clicks += l.clicks ?? 0;\n    acc.conversions += l.conversions ?? 0;\n    linksByContent.set(l.content_id, acc);\n  }\n\n  const byCreative = new Map<string, PautaEntry>();\n  for (const m of metrics) {\n    if (!m.content_variant_id) continue;\n    const prev = byCreative.get(m.content_variant_id);\n    const entry: PautaEntry = prev ?? {\n      contentId: m.content_variant_id,\n      impressions: 0,\n      engagements: 0,\n      engagementRate: 0,\n      hookRate: null,\n      affiliateClicks: 0,\n      revenueCents: 0,\n      platforms: [],\n      productId: null,\n      status: 'candidato',\n      effort: 'medium',\n      lastCollectedAt: m.collected_at,\n    };\n    entry.impressions += m.impressions ?? 0;\n    entry.engagements += m.engagements ?? 0;\n    entry.affiliateClicks += m.affiliate_clicks ?? 0;\n    entry.revenueCents += m.revenue_cents ?? 0;\n    if (m.hook_rate != null) entry.hookRate = Math.max(entry.hookRate ?? 0, m.hook_rate);\n    if (m.platform && !entry.platforms.includes(m.platform)) entry.platforms.push(m.platform);\n    if (m.product_id && !entry.productId) entry.productId = m.product_id;\n    if (m.collected_at > entry.lastCollectedAt) entry.lastCollectedAt = m.collected_at;\n    byCreative.set(m.content_variant_id, entry);\n  }\n\n  const entries = [...byCreative.values()];\n  for (const e of entries) {\n    const link = linksByContent.get(e.contentId);\n    e.revenueCents += link?.revenue ?? 0;\n    e.engagementRate = e.impressions > 0 ? e.engagements / e.impressions : 0;\n    e.status = e.revenueCents > 0 ? 'convertendo' : link ? 'monetizado' : 'candidato';\n    e.effort = e.productId ? 'low' : 'medium';\n  }\n\n  return entries.sort((a, b) => {\n    const s = STATUS_ORDER[a.status] - STATUS_ORDER[b.status];\n    if (s !== 0) return s;\n    if (b.engagementRate !== a.engagementRate) return b.engagementRate - a.engagementRate;\n    if (b.engagements !== a.engagements) return b.engagements - a.engagements;\n    return b.impressions - a.impressions;\n  });\n}\n"
}
```

  "new_string": "export interface RevenuePautaData {\n  entries: PautaEntry[];\n  titles: Record<string, string>;\n}\n\n// FR-VA-031 \u2014 Revenue Opportunity Pauta (HITL). Read-only: real collected metrics + link status;\n// ranking is pure (src/lib/revenue-pauta.ts). The monetization decision stays with the Sovereign.\nexport function useRevenuePauta(limit = 8) {\n  const { user } = useAuth();\n  return useQuery({\n    queryKey: ['revenue-pauta', limit],\n    enabled: !!user,\n    staleTime: 60_000,\n    queryFn: async (): Promise<RevenuePautaData> => {\n      const [metricsRes, linksRes] = await Promise.all([\n        supabase\n          .from('creative_metrics')\n          .select('content_variant_id, impressions, engagements, hook_rate, affiliate_clicks, revenue_cents, platform, product_id, collected_at')\n          .order('collected_at', { ascending: false })\n          .limit(500),\n        supabase\n          .from('affiliate_links')\n          .select('content_id, revenue_cents, clicks, conversions')\n          .not('content_id', 'is', null),\n      ]);\n      if (metricsRes.error) throw metricsRes.error;\n      if (linksRes.error) throw linksRes.error;\n      const entries = rankRevenueCandidates(\n        (metricsRes.data ?? []) as CreativeMetricRow[],\n        (linksRes.data ?? []) as AffiliateLinkRow[],\n      ).slice(0, limit);\n\n      let titles: Record<string, string> = {};\n      const ids = entries.map((e) => e.contentId);\n      if (ids.length > 0) {\n        const { data: rows, error } = await supabase\n          .from('content_library').select('id, title').in('id', ids);\n        if (error) throw error;\n        titles = Object.fromEntries((rows ?? []).map((r) => [r.id, r.title ?? '(sem t\u00edtulo)']));\n      }\n      return { entries, titles };\n    },\n  });\n}\n\nexport interface RunResult {"
}
```

>   upload <sel> <files...>    Upload files
>   download <sel> <path>      Download file by clicking element
>   scroll <dir> [px]          Scroll (up/down/left/right)
>   scrollintoview <sel>       Scroll element into view
>   wait <sel|ms>              Wait for element or time
>   screenshot [path]          Take screenshot
>   pdf <path>                 Save as PDF
>   snapshot                   Accessibility tree with refs (for AI)
>   eval <js>                  Run JavaScript
>   connect <port|url>         Connect to browser via CDP
>   close [--all]              Close browser (--all closes every session)
> 
> Navigation:
>   back                       Go back
>   forward                    Go forward
>   reload                     Reload page
> 
> Get Info:  agent-browser get <what> [selector]
>   text, html, value, attr <name>, title, url, count, box, styles, cdp-url
> 
> Check State:  agent-browser is <what> <selector>
>   visible, enabled, checked
> 
> Find Elements:  agent-browser find <locator> <value> <action> [text]
>   role, text, label, placeholder, alt, title, testid, first, last, nth
> 
> Mouse:  agent-browser mouse <action> [args]
>   move <x> <y>, down [btn], up [btn], wheel <dy> [dx]
> 
> Browser Settings:  agent-browser set <setting> [value]
>   viewport <w> <h>, device <name>, geo <lat> <lng>
>   offline [on|off], headers <json>, credentials <user> <pass>
>   media [dark|light] [reduced-motion]
> 
> Network:  agent-browser network <action>
>   route <url> [--abort|--body <json>] [--resource-type <csv>]
>   unroute [url]
>   requests [--clear] [--filter <pattern>]
>   har <start|stop> [path]
> 
> Storage:
>   cookies [get|set|clear]    Manage cookies (set supports --url, --domain, --path, --httpOnly, --secure, --sameSite, --expires)
>                              Or:  cookies set --curl <file> [--domain <host>] (auto-detects JSON/cURL/Cookie-header files)
>   storage <local|session>    Manage web storage
> 
> Tabs:
> ```

## PHASE 1 — SECURITY AUDIT

Run these checks and report findings. **Block the seal if any CRITICAL finding exists.**

> ⚠️ **SCOPE NOTE:** This phase scans changes that exist NOW (before Phase 5 writes HANDOFF.md).
> A second mandatory scan runs in Phase 5b, after HANDOFF.md is written and before it is committed.

```bash
# 1a. Secret leak scan — check staged + working tree for hardcoded credentials
git diff HEAD | grep -E "(sk-[a-zA-Z0-9]{20,}|AIza[0-9A-Za-z_-]{35}|eyJ[a-zA-Z0-9._-]{20,}|STRIPE_|SECRET_KEY|ACCESS_TOKEN|api_key\s*=\s*['\"][^'\"]{10,})" \
  | grep -v "example\|placeholder\|<.*>\|your-key\|YOUR_" | head -20

# 1b. Edge function JWT enforcement — every user-facing function must verify JWT
grep -rL "Authorization\|jwt\|JWT\|service_role" supabase/functions/*/index.ts \
  | grep -v "get-infra-status\|watchdog" || echo "ALL FUNCTIONS: JWT enforced ✅"

# 1c. Client-side coin deduction guard — must NEVER update mco_balance directly from client
grep -rn "mco_balance.*update\|UPDATE.*mco_balance" src/ \
  | grep -v "settings\|top.up\|topup\|SettingsPage" | head -10 || echo "NO VIOLATIONS ✅"

# 1d. RLS bypass risk — check for supabase.rpc calls without auth context
grep -rn "service_role\|bypass.*rls\|rls.*bypass" src/ | head -10 || echo "NO VIOLATIONS ✅"

# 1e. Sensitive data in console.log
grep -rn "console\.log.*token\|console\.log.*key\|console\.log.*secret\|console\.log.*password" src/ \
  | head -10 || echo "NO SENSITIVE LOGS ✅"
```

**Security verdict:** list each check as ✅ PASS / ⚠️ WARN / 🔴 BLOCK.
Only proceed if no 🔴 findings.

### DOCUMENTATION CREDENTIAL RULE (invariant — never violate)

**When writing HANDOFF.md, README.md, or any committed documentation:**

- ✅ DO: describe WHERE a credential is stored → `VITE_GEMINI_API_KEY` is set in `.env` (local) and `GEMINI_API_KEY` in the Supabase vault
- ✅ DO: reference digest/fingerprint if useful → `GEMINI_API_KEY (digest 0fe0e159...)`
- 🔴 NEVER: include the actual credential value → `AIzaSyBv...`, `sk-...`, `eyJ...`

> **Rationale:** Phase 1 scans changes that predate the HANDOFF.md write. Any credential embedded
> in HANDOFF.md during Phase 5 escapes Phase 1 entirely and goes directly to the remote.
> This rule is the primary prevention layer; Phase 5b is the detection backstop.

Insert a milestone node into mcorch_nodes to mark this session in the knowledge graph:

```bash
source .env
SUPABASE_URL="https://bcyvddsykvehvpwstlfa.supabase.co"
SESSION_PHASE="<phase-name-slug>"
SESSION_SUMMARY="<one-sentence summary of what was accomplished>"

curl -s -X POST "${SUPABASE_URL}/rest/v1/mcorch_nodes" \
  -H "apikey: ${SB_SECRET_KEY}" \
  -H "Authorization: Bearer ${SB_SECRET_KEY}" \
  -H "Content-Type: application/json" \
  -H "Prefer: return=representation" \
  -d "{
    \"name\": \"session-handoff-$(date +%Y%m%d)-${SESSION_PHASE}\",
    \"node_type\": \"handoff\",
    \"content\": \"[HANDOFF SEAL] ${SESSION_SUMMARY}\",
    \"stability_score\": 1.0,
    \"project_id\": null,
    \"user_id\": null,
    \"metadata\": {
      \"session\": \"${SESSION_PHASE}\",
      \"sealed_at\": \"$(date -u +%Y-%m-%dT%H:%M:%SZ)\",
      \"commits\": \"$(git log --oneline -5 | head -5 | tr '\n' '|')\"
    }
  }" | python3 -c "import sys,json; d=json.load(sys.stdin); print('Node ID:', d[0]['id'] if isinstance(d,list) else d.get('id','error'))" 2>/dev/null \
  || echo "⚠️ Knowledge Mesh insert failed — log manually"
```

Then trigger embedding for the new node:
```bash
NODE_ID="<id-from-above>"
curl -s -X POST "${SUPABASE_URL}/functions/v1/embed-mcorch-node" \
  -H "apikey: ${SB_SECRET_KEY}" \
  -H "Authorization: Bearer ${SB_SECRET_KEY}" \
  -H "Content-Type: application/json" \
  -d "{\"record\": {\"id\": \"${NODE_ID}\"}}" | python3 -c "import sys,json; d=json.load(sys.stdin); print('Embedded:', d)" 2>/dev/null \
  || echo "⚠️ Embedding failed — node persisted but not vectorized"
```

Emit `proof-manifest.json` so Phase 5c can verify this handoff node strictly:
```bash

```

  | head -20 || echo "HANDOFF.md: NO CREDENTIALS FOUND ✅"

# 5b-2. Specifically check for Google API keys (AIza prefix = 39 chars total)

## PHASE 6 — BoK SEAL STATUS (mandatory before push)

**Verify any significant module work in this session has a sealed BoK suite per CLAUDE.md MCORCH Master Execution Protocol.**

```bash
# 6-1. List BoK suites present + check completeness
for slug in $(ls docs/bok/ 2>/dev/null); do
  required=(00-index 01-mrd 02-brd 03-prd 04-frd 05-sdd 06-data-model 07-process-flow 08-quality-metrics)
  missing=()
  for doc in "${required[@]}"; do
    [ -f "docs/bok/$slug/$doc.md" ] || missing+=("$doc")
  done
  [ ${#missing[@]} -eq 0 ] \
    && echo "✅ $slug — BoK complete (9 docs)" \
    || echo "⚠️ $slug — incomplete (missing: ${missing[*]})"
done

# 6-2. Check session touched code in new src/pages/ or new supabase/functions/ that lacks BoK
session_new_modules=$(git diff --name-only HEAD~10..HEAD 2>/dev/null | grep -E "^(src/pages/|supabase/functions/|src/components/[A-Z])" | awk -F/ '{print incremental"/"do}' | sort -u)
echo "Session touched modules: $session_new_modules"

# 6-3. Mesh seal nodes for sealed BoK suites
source .env 2>/dev/null
if [ -n "$SB_SECRET_KEY" ]; then
  curl -s "$SUPABASE_URL/rest/v1/mcorch_nodes?node_type=eq.documentation_suite&select=id,name,stability_score,created_at" \
    -H "apikey: $SB_SECRET_KEY" \
    -H "Authorization: Bearer $SB_SECRET_KEY" \
    | python3 -c "import sys,json; d=json.load(sys.stdin); [print(f\"  🌐 {n['name']} ({n['stability_score']})\") for n in d]" 2>/dev/null \
    || echo "  ⚠️ Could not query seal nodes"
fi
```

**Gate verdict:**
- ✅ All session modules have sealed BoK → proceed to Phase 7 push.
- ⚠️ Module work without BoK (e.g. `feat:` commit creating new page/edge fn without `docs/bok/<slug>/`)
  → 🔴 **BLOCK seal**. Run `/bok-scribe <module>` first OR add module to exemption list (typo/dep bump/single-file patch).

Report BoK status in final seal output (Phase 8).

**Invariants — never skip:**
- Security audit must run and report before any commit (Phase 1)
- **Phase 3 (milestone node) must run BEFORE Phase 4 (README badge query)** — ensures count includes the handoff node
- **Phase 5b secret scan must run on HANDOFF.md before its commit** — no exceptions
- **Phase 5c material proof audit must run and return `SEAL ALLOWED` before push** — a contradicted claim blocks the seal
- **Documentation must NEVER contain actual credential values** — only describe where they live
- README.md badge counts must reflect real DB state (query live, after milestone insert)
- HANDOFF.md must be updated even if nothing changed (session note)
- Push must happen — remote must always mirror local main
- Knowledge Mesh milestone must be attempted (failures are logged, not blocking)
- git pre-commit hook (`.git/hooks/pre-commit`) is the last barrier — do not bypass with `--no-verify`

## 👤 User *(14:02:00)*

# /loop — schedule a recurring or self-paced prompt

Parse the input below into `[interval] <prompt…>` and schedule it.

## Parsing (in priority order)

1. **Leading token**: if the first whitespace-delimited token matches `^\d+[smhd]$` (e.g. `5m`, `2h`), that's the interval; the rest is the prompt.
2. **Trailing "every" clause**: otherwise, if the input ends with `every <N><unit>` or `every <N> <unit-word>` (e.g. `every 20m`, `every 5 minutes`, `every 2 hours`), extract that as the interval and strip it from the prompt. Only match when what follows "every" is a time expression — `check every PR` has no interval.
3. **No interval**: otherwise, the entire input is the prompt and you'll self-pace dynamically (see "Dynamic mode" below).

If the resulting prompt is empty, show usage `/loop [interval] <prompt>` and stop.

Examples:
- `5m /babysit-prs` → interval `5m`, prompt `/babysit-prs` (rule 1)
- `check the deploy every 20m` → interval `20m`, prompt `check the deploy` (rule 2)
- `run tests every 5 minutes` → interval `5m`, prompt `run tests` (rule 2)
- `check the deploy` → no interval → dynamic mode, prompt `check the deploy` (rule 3)
- `check every PR` → no interval → dynamic mode, prompt `check every PR` (rule 3 — "every" not followed by time)
- `5m` → empty prompt → show usage

## Offer cloud first

Before any scheduling step, check whether EITHER is true:
- the parsed interval (rule 1 or 2) is **≥60 minutes**, or
- regardless of which rule matched, the original input uses daily phrasing ("every morning", "daily", "every day", "each night", "every weekday")

If either is true, call AskUserQuestion first:
- `question`: "This loop stops when you close this session. Set it up as a cloud schedule instead so it keeps running?"
- `header`: "Schedule"
- `options`: `[{label: "Cloud schedule (recommended)", description: "Runs in Anthropic's cloud even after you close this session"}, {label: "This session only", description: "Runs in this terminal until you exit"}]`

If they pick **Cloud schedule**: do NOT call CronCreate. Invoke the `schedule` skill directly via the Skill tool with `args` set to their original input verbatim (e.g. `Skill({skill: "schedule", args: "every morning tell me a joke"})`), then follow that skill's instructions to completion. Do NOT tell the user to run /schedule themselves. **Then stop — do not continue to any section below** (no CronCreate, no ScheduleWakeup, no "execute the prompt now").
If they pick **This session only**:
- If the trigger was a parsed ≥60-minute interval (rule 1 or 2): continue below with that interval.
- If the trigger was daily phrasing only (rule 3, no parsed interval): do NOT call CronCreate. Explain that a daily-cadence loop won't fire before this session closes, so there's nothing useful to schedule locally — suggest they either pick Cloud schedule, or re-run `/loop` with an explicit shorter interval (e.g. `/loop 1h <prompt>`) if they want a session loop. Then stop.
If neither trigger condition was met: continue below.

## Fixed-interval mode (rules 1 and 2)

Convert the interval to a cron expression:

| Interval pattern      | Cron expression     | Notes                                    |
|-----------------------|---------------------|------------------------------------------|
| `Nm` where N ≤ 59   | `*/N * * * *`     | every N minutes                          |
| `Nm` where N ≥ 60   | `0 */H * * *`     | round to hours (H = N/60, must divide 24)|
| `Nh` where N ≤ 23   | `0 */N * * *`     | every N hours                            |
| `Nd`                | `0 0 */N * *`     | every N days at midnight local           |
| `Ns`                | treat as `ceil(N/60)m` | cron minimum granularity is 1 minute  |

**If the interval doesn't cleanly divide its unit** (e.g. `7m` → `*/7 * * * *` gives uneven gaps at :56→:00; `90m` → 1.5h which cron can't express), pick the nearest clean interval and tell the user what you rounded to before scheduling.

Then:
1. Call CronCreate with: `cron` (the expression above), `prompt` (the parsed prompt verbatim), `recurring: true`.
2. Briefly confirm: what's scheduled, the cron expression, the human-readable cadence, that recurring tasks auto-expire after 7 days, and that the user can cancel sooner with CronDelete (include the job ID). Only if you did NOT show the cloud-offer AskUserQuestion above (i.e., neither trigger condition applied), end the confirmation with this exact line on its own, italicized: `_Runs until you close this session · For durable cloud-based loops, use /schedule_`. If the user already answered that question, omit this line.
3. **Then immediately execute the parsed prompt now** — don't wait for the first cron fire. If it's a slash command, invoke it via the Skill tool; otherwise act on it directly.

## Dynamic mode (rule 3 — no interval)

The user wants you to self-pace. Decide what makes the next iteration worth running — a passage of time, or an observable event.

1. **Run the parsed prompt now.** If it's a slash command, invoke it via the Skill tool; otherwise act on it directly.
2. **If the next run is gated on an event** (CI finishing, a log line matching, a file changing, a PR comment) and no Monitor is already running for it: arm one now with `persistent: true`. Its events arrive as `<task-notification>` messages and wake this loop immediately — you do not wait for the ScheduleWakeup deadline. Arm once; on later iterations call TaskList first and skip this step if a monitor is already running.
3. **Briefly confirm**: that you're self-pacing, whether a Monitor is the primary wake signal, that you ran the task now, and what fallback delay you're about to pick. Write this as text *before* calling ScheduleWakeup — the turn ends as soon as that tool returns.
4. **Then, as the last action of this turn, call ScheduleWakeup** with:
   - `delaySeconds`: with a Monitor armed this is the **fallback heartbeat** — how long to wait if no event fires (lean 1200–1800s; idle ticks past the 5-minute cache window are pure overhead). Without a Monitor this is the cadence — pick based on what you observed. Read the tool's own description for cache-aware delay guidance.
   - `reason`: one short sentence on why you picked that delay.
   - `prompt`: the full original /loop input verbatim, prefixed with `/loop ` so the next firing re-enters this skill and continues the loop. For example, if the user typed `/loop check the deploy`, pass `/loop check the deploy` as the prompt.
5. **If you were woken by a `<task-notification>`** rather than this prompt: handle the event in the context of the loop task, then call ScheduleWakeup again with the same `prompt` and the same 1200–1800s `delaySeconds` from step 4 — the Monitor remains the wake signal; this only resets the safety net.
6. **To stop the loop**, omit the ScheduleWakeup call and TaskStop any Monitor you armed (use TaskList to find the task ID if it is no longer in context). Before you stop, send a one-line outcome via PushNotification — the user may be away and waiting to hear it's done. Skip this if you're stopping because the user just told you to; they're already here.

Reporte em PT-BR cada iteração.

> 5	// affiliate_links.clicks/revenue_cents, joined by content_id = content_variant_id), ranks the winning
> 6	// product + platform, and writes an optimization_policy that the generator injects next cycle (FR-VA-011).
> 7	//
> 8	// Identity: service-role-only (403 otherwise). The tenant is DERIVED FROM previous_cycle_id (autopilot_cycles
> 9	// row), NEVER from the body (anti-IDOR; same principle as finalize_vision_job). Billing: ANALYZE_COST(2) is
> 10	// charged ONLY when has_real_data AND NOT prepaid (the cycle pre-reserved the 2 — FR-VA-007). No real data →
> 11	// 0 mco + 'analyze_empty' telemetry, and NO policy is invented.
> 12	import { createClient } from "https://esm.sh/@supabase/supabase-js@2";
> 13	
> 14	const corsHeaders = {
> 15	  "Access-Control-Allow-Origin": "*",
> 16	  "Access-Control-Allow-Methods": "POST, OPTIONS",
> 17	  "Access-Control-Allow-Headers": "authorization, x-client-info, apikey, content-type",
> 18	};
> 19	const ANALYZE_COST = 2;
> 20	const UUID_RE = /^[0-9a-fA-F-]{36}$/;
> 21	
> 22	Deno.serve(async (req) => {
> 23	  if (req.method === "OPTIONS") return new Response("ok", { headers: corsHeaders });
> 24	  const json = (b: unknown, s = 200) =>
> 25	    new Response(JSON.stringify(b), { status: s, headers: { ...corsHeaders, "Content-Type": "application/json" } });
> 26	
> 27	  const url = Deno.env.get("SUPABASE_URL")!;
> 28	  const serviceKey = (Deno.env.get("SB_SECRET_KEY") ?? Deno.env.get("SUPABASE_SERVICE_ROLE_KEY"))!;
> 29	  if (req.headers.get("Authorization") !== `Bearer ${serviceKey}`) return json({ error: "Forbidden" }, 403);
> 30	  const admin = createClient(url, serviceKey);
> 31	  const logEvent = (event: string) =>
> 32	    admin.from("infra_health_logs").insert({ service: "autopilot-analyze", status: "healthy", event }).then(() => {}, () => {});
> 33	
> 34	  try {
> 35	    const body = await req.json().catch(() => ({}));
> 36	    const prevCycleId = typeof body?.previous_cycle_id === "string" ? body.previous_cycle_id : null;
> 37	    const prepaid = body?.prepaid === true;
> 38	    if (!prevCycleId || !UUID_RE.test(prevCycleId)) return json({ error: "previous_cycle_id (uuid) required" }, 422);
> 39	
> 40	    // Tenant + plan DERIVED from the trusted cycle row (never the body).
> 41	    const { data: cyc } = await admin.from("autopilot_cycles").select("user_id, plan_id, started_at").eq("id", prevCycleId).maybeSingle();
> 42	    if (!cyc) return json({ error: "cycle_not_found" }, 404);
> 43	    const userId = cyc.user_id as string;
> 44	    const planId = cyc.plan_id as string;
> 45	
> 46	    // B4 — EWMA multi-cycle window (FRD v0.3 embedded fix of FR-VA-010/011; SDD: "R sobre o ciclo (EWMA M
> 47	    // ciclos)"). Single-cycle reads made the policy thrash on one noisy cycle; the reward now aggregates the
> 48	    // plan's last M cycles up to the analyzed one, each weighted by EWMA_DECAY^age (age 0 = the analyzed
> 49	    // cycle): recent evidence dominates, older cycles stabilize the ranking. Consequence (documented): a
> 50	    // zero-signal cycle under a plan WITH history still yields a policy — the window remembers; only a plan
> 51	    // whose whole window is empty returns has_real_data=false. M/decay are code constants until the
> 52	    // plan-level reward_weights config-as-data column lands (NFR-VA-010, deferred with the reward weights).
> 53	    const EWMA_M = 5;
> 54	    const EWMA_DECAY = 0.5;
> 55	    const { data: windowCycles } = await admin.from("autopilot_cycles")
> 56	      .select("id")
> 57	      .eq("plan_id", planId).eq("user_id", userId)
> 58	      .lte("started_at", cyc.started_at as string)
> 59	      .order("started_at", { ascending: false }).limit(EWMA_M);
> 60	    const cycleWeight = new Map<string, number>();
> 61	    ((windowCycles ?? []) as Array<{ id: string }>).forEach((c, i) => cycleWeight.set(c.id, Math.pow(EWMA_DECAY, i)));
> 62	    if (!cycleWeight.has(prevCycleId)) cycleWeight.set(prevCycleId, 1);
> 63	    const cycleIds = [...cycleWeight.keys()];
> 64	
> 65	    // MULTI-METRIC reward (FR-VA-029, brand-first): clicks alone is a Goodhart proxy (clickbait wins). The reward
> 66	    // R combines REACH + ENGAGEMENT-RATE + BRAND + REVENUE, max-normalized within the candidate set:
> 67	    //   R = 0.40·reach + 0.30·eng_rate + 0.20·brand + 0.10·revenue
> 68	    // Social outcome (reach/eng/brand) comes from publication_metrics (FR-VA-027); affiliate (clicks/rev) from
> 69	    // creative_metrics. HONEST DEGRADATION (Lei 1): when NO social data has flowed yet (reach/eng all 0 — e.g. before
> 70	    // the IG insights re-OAuth), R falls back to the affiliate signal and records mode='fallback' in reward_vector —
> 71	    // never fabricates a reach number.
> 72	    const W = { reach: 0.40, eng: 0.30, brand: 0.20, rev: 0.10 };
> 73	    const { data: metrics } = await admin.from("creative_metrics")
> 74	      .select("cycle_id, content_variant_id, product_id, platform, affiliate_clicks, revenue_cents, impressions, engagements")
> 75	      .in("cycle_id", cycleIds).eq("user_id", userId);
> 76	    const rows = (metrics ?? []) as Array<{ cycle_id: string; content_variant_id: string | null; product_id: string | null; platform: string | null; affiliate_clicks: number | null; revenue_cents: number | null; impressions: number | null; engagements: number | null }>;
> 77	
> 78	    // Latest publication_metrics snapshot per (content_variant_id, platform) for the cycle's creatives.
> 79	    const variantIds = [...new Set(rows.map((r) => r.content_variant_id).filter(Boolean))] as string[];
> 80	    const pubByVariant = new Map<string, { reach: number; eng: number; brand: number }>();
> 81	    if (variantIds.length) {
> 82	      const { data: pubs } = await admin.from("publication_metrics")
> 83	        .select("content_variant_id, platform, reach, likes, comments, shares, saves, snapshot_at")
> 84	        .in("content_variant_id", variantIds).order("snapshot_at", { ascending: false });
> 85	      const seen = new Set<string>();
> 86	      for (const p of (pubs ?? []) as Array<Record<string, number | string>>) {
> 87	        const k = `${p.content_variant_id}:${p.platform}`;
> 88	        if (seen.has(k)) continue; seen.add(k);   // keep only the latest snapshot per post
> 89	        const eng = Number(p.likes ?? 0) + Number(p.comments ?? 0) + Number(p.shares ?? 0) + Number(p.saves ?? 0);
> 90	        const brand = Number(p.comments ?? 0) + Number(p.shares ?? 0) + Number(p.saves ?? 0);
> 91	        const cur = pubByVariant.get(p.content_variant_id as string) ?? { reach: 0, eng: 0, brand: 0 };
> 92	        cur.reach += Number(p.reach ?? 0); cur.eng += eng; cur.brand += brand;
> 93	        pubByVariant.set(p.content_variant_id as string, cur);
> 94	      }
> 95	    }
> 96	
> 97	    type Agg = { reach: number; eng: number; brand: number; rev: number; clicks: number; impr: number };
> 98	    const blank = (): Agg => ({ reach: 0, eng: 0, brand: 0, rev: 0, clicks: 0, impr: 0 });
> 99	    const byProduct = new Map<string, Agg>();
> 100	    const byPlatform = new Map<string, Agg>();
> 101	    const add = (m: Map<string, Agg>, k: string | null, a: Agg) => {
> 102	      if (!k) return; const e = m.get(k) ?? blank();
> 103	      e.reach += a.reach; e.eng += a.eng; e.brand += a.brand; e.rev += a.rev; e.clicks += a.clicks; e.impr += a.impr; m.set(k, e);
> 104	    };
> 105	    let totalClicks = 0, totalRev = 0, totalReach = 0, totalEng = 0;
> 106	    for (const r of rows) {
> 107	      const pub = (r.content_variant_id && pubByVariant.get(r.content_variant_id)) || { reach: 0, eng: 0, brand: 0 };
> 108	      const w = cycleWeight.get(r.cycle_id) ?? 0;   // EWMA: the row's cycle age sets its weight
> 109	      const a: Agg = {
> 110	        reach: pub.reach * w, eng: (pub.eng || Number(r.engagements ?? 0)) * w, brand: pub.brand * w,
> 111	        rev: Number(r.revenue_cents ?? 0) * w, clicks: Number(r.affiliate_clicks ?? 0) * w, impr: Number(r.impressions ?? 0) * w,
> 112	      };
> 113	      add(byProduct, r.product_id, a); add(byPlatform, r.platform, a);
> 114	      totalClicks += a.clicks; totalRev += a.rev; totalReach += a.reach; totalEng += a.eng;
> 115	    }
> 116	
> 117	    const hasRealData = totalClicks > 0 || totalRev > 0 || totalReach > 0 || totalEng > 0;
> 118	    if (!hasRealData) {
> 119	      logEvent("autopilot_analyze_empty");
> 120	      return json({ ok: true, has_real_data: false, charged: 0 });
> 121	    }
> 122	
> 123	    // R: brand-first multi-metric when social data exists; honest affiliate fallback otherwise.
> 124	    const socialLive = totalReach > 0 || totalEng > 0;
> 125	    const rewardFn = (m: Map<string, Agg>) => {
> 126	      const vals = [...m.values()];
> 127	      const max = {
> 128	        reach: Math.max(1, ...vals.map((v) => v.reach)),
> 129	        engRate: Math.max(1e-9, ...vals.map((v) => v.eng / Math.max(v.reach, v.impr, 1))),
> 130	        brand: Math.max(1, ...vals.map((v) => v.brand)),
> 131	        rev: Math.max(1, ...vals.map((v) => v.rev)),
> 132	        clicks: Math.max(1, ...vals.map((v) => v.clicks)),
> 133	      };
> 134	      return (a: Agg) => socialLive
> 135	        ? W.reach * (a.reach / max.reach) + W.eng * ((a.eng / Math.max(a.reach, a.impr, 1)) / max.engRate)
> 136	          + W.brand * (a.brand / max.brand) + W.rev * (a.rev / max.rev)
> 137	        : (a.clicks / max.clicks) * 0.9 + (a.rev / max.rev) * 0.1;   // fallback: affiliate signal (no social yet)
> 138	    };
> 139	    const pickBy = (m: Map<string, Agg>) => { const R = rewardFn(m); return [...m.entries()].sort((x, y) => R(y[1]) - R(x[1]))[0] ?? null; };
> 140	    const topProd = pickBy(byProduct);
> 141	    const topPlat = pickBy(byPlatform);
> 142	    const topProductId = topProd?.[0] ?? null;
> 143	    const topPlatform = topPlat?.[0] ?? null;
> 144	    const winnerR = topProd ? rewardFn(byProduct)(topProd[1]) : 0;
> 145	
> 146	    let topProductName: string | null = null;
> 147	    if (topProductId) {
> 148	      const { data: p } = await admin.from("vm_affiliate_products").select("name").eq("external_id", topProductId).maybeSingle();
> 149	      topProductName = (p as { name?: string } | null)?.name ?? null;
> 150	    }
> 151	
> 152	    const reward_vector = topProd ? {
> 153	      reach: topProd[1].reach, eng_rate: topProd[1].eng / Math.max(topProd[1].reach, topProd[1].impr, 1),
> 154	      brand: topProd[1].brand, revenue_cents: topProd[1].rev, clicks: topProd[1].clicks, weights: W,
> 155	      mode: socialLive ? "brand_first_multimetric" : "fallback_affiliate_no_social_data_yet",
> 156	      ewma: { m: EWMA_M, decay: EWMA_DECAY, cycles_used: cycleIds.length },   // B4 audit trail
> 157	    } : null;
> 158	    const actionDetails = {
> 159	      top_product_id: topProductId, top_product_name: topProductName, top_platform: topPlatform,
> 160	      signal: socialLive ? "multimetric_R" : "affiliate_clicks", reward_vector,
> 161	      clicks_total: totalClicks, revenue_cents_total: totalRev, reach_total: totalReach, eng_total: totalEng,
> 162	    };
> 163	    const { data: pol } = await admin.from("optimization_policy").insert({
> 164	      user_id: userId, action_type: "viral_autopilot", action_details: actionDetails,
> 165	      reward: winnerR, context: { scope: `viral_autopilot:${planId}`, plan_id: planId, cycle_id: prevCycleId }, applied: false,
> 166	    }).select("id").single();
> 167	
> 168	    // Self-bill ONLY on the standalone path; the inline (prepaid) path was pre-reserved by the cycle.
> 169	    let charged = 0;
> 170	    if (!prepaid) {
> 171	      const { error: dErr } = await admin.rpc("deduct_mco_coins", {
> 172	        p_user_id: userId, p_amount: ANALYZE_COST, p_action: "autopilot_analyze", p_context: { cycle_id: prevCycleId },
> 173	      });
> 174	      if (dErr) return json({ error: "insufficient_balance", detail: dErr.message }, 402);
> 175	      charged = ANALYZE_COST;
> 176	    }
> 177	
> 178	    logEvent("autopilot_analyze_done");
> 179	    return json({ ok: true, has_real_data: true, charged, policy_id: pol?.id ?? null, policy: actionDetails });
> 180	  } catch (e) {
> 181	    return json({ error: e instanceof Error ? e.message : "unknown" }, 500);
> 182	  }
> 183	});
> 184	
> ```

> # drift) and do NOT flip the exit code — only true drift (ledger / out-of-band object / RPC contract

  "old_string": "const ANALYZE_COST = 2;\nconst UUID_RE = /^[0-9a-fA-F-]{36}$/;",
  "new_string": "const ANALYZE_COST = 2;\nconst UUID_RE = /^[0-9a-fA-F-]{36}$/;\n\n// NFR-VA-010 \u2014 reward config as per-plan DATA (autopilot_plans.reward_weights jsonb; SOP Amendment (c)).\n// The column is RLS-own user input consumed only as numeric ranking weights \u2014 validate hard, never 500:\n// the 4 weights must be finite numbers >= 0 with sum > 0 (then NORMALIZED to sum 1 \u2014 users write\n// proportions); ewma_m int 1..12; ewma_decay in (0,1). Anything off-shape falls back to the engine\n// defaults with weights_source='default_invalid' (fail-safe + auditable in reward_vector).\ntype RewardW = { reach: number; eng: number; brand: number; rev: number };\nconst DEFAULT_W: RewardW = { reach: 0.40, eng: 0.30, brand: 0.20, rev: 0.10 };\nconst DEFAULT_EWMA_M = 5;\nconst DEFAULT_EWMA_DECAY = 0.5;\ntype RewardConfig = { W: RewardW; ewmaM: number; ewmaDecay: number; source: \"plan\" | \"default\" | \"default_invalid\" };\nfunction resolveRewardConfig(raw: unknown): RewardConfig {\n  const defaults = (source: RewardConfig[\"source\"]): RewardConfig =>\n    ({ W: DEFAULT_W, ewmaM: DEFAULT_EWMA_M, ewmaDecay: DEFAULT_EWMA_DECAY, source });\n  if (raw == null) return defaults(\"default\");\n  if (typeof raw !== \"object\" || Array.isArray(raw)) return defaults(\"default_invalid\");\n  const o = raw as Record<string, unknown>;\n  const num = (v: unknown): number | null =>\n    typeof v === \"number\" && Number.isFinite(v) && v >= 0 ? v : null;\n  const reach = num(o.reach), eng = num(o.eng), brand = num(o.brand), rev = num(o.rev);\n  if (reach == null || eng == null || brand == null || rev == null) return defaults(\"default_invalid\");\n  const sum = reach + eng + brand + rev;\n  if (sum <= 0) return defaults(\"default_invalid\");\n  const ewmaM = Number.isInteger(o.ewma_m) && (o.ewma_m as number) >= 1 && (o.ewma_m as number) <= 12\n    ? (o.ewma_m as number) : DEFAULT_EWMA_M;\n  const ewmaDecay = typeof o.ewma_decay === \"number\" && o.ewma_decay > 0 && o.ewma_decay < 1\n    ? o.ewma_decay : DEFAULT_EWMA_DECAY;\n  return { W: { reach: reach / sum, eng: eng / sum, brand: brand / sum, rev: rev / sum }, ewmaM, ewmaDecay, source: \"plan\" };\n}"
}

OBJECTIVE:
Perform a security-focused code review to identify HIGH-CONFIDENCE security vulnerabilities that could have real exploitation potential. This is not a general code review - focus ONLY on security implications newly added by this PR. Do not comment on existing security concerns.

CRITICAL INSTRUCTIONS:
1. MINIMIZE FALSE POSITIVES: Only flag issues where you're >80% confident of actual exploitability
2. AVOID NOISE: Skip theoretical issues, style concerns, or low-impact findings
3. FOCUS ON IMPACT: Prioritize vulnerabilities that could lead to unauthorized access, data breaches, or system compromise
4. EXCLUSIONS: Do NOT report the following issue types:
   - Denial of Service (DOS) vulnerabilities, even if they allow service disruption
   - Secrets or sensitive data stored on disk (these are handled by other processes)
   - Rate limiting or resource exhaustion issues

SECURITY CATEGORIES TO EXAMINE:

**Input Validation Vulnerabilities:**
- SQL injection via unsanitized user input
- Command injection in system calls or subprocesses
- XXE injection in XML parsing
- Template injection in templating engines
- NoSQL injection in database queries
- Path traversal in file operations

**Authentication & Authorization Issues:**
- Authentication bypass logic
- Privilege escalation paths
- Session management flaws
- JWT token vulnerabilities
- Authorization logic bypasses

**Crypto & Secrets Management:**
- Hardcoded API keys, passwords, or tokens
- Weak cryptographic algorithms or implementations
- Improper key storage or management
- Cryptographic randomness issues
- Certificate validation bypasses

**Injection & Code Execution:**
- Remote code execution via deseralization
- Pickle injection in Python
- YAML deserialization vulnerabilities
- Eval injection in dynamic code execution
- XSS vulnerabilities in web applications (reflected, stored, DOM-based)

**Data Exposure:**
- Sensitive data logging or storage
- PII handling violations
- API endpoint data leakage
- Debug information exposure

Additional notes:
- Even if something is only exploitable from the local network, it can still be a HIGH severity issue

ANALYSIS METHODOLOGY:

Phase 1 - Repository Context Research (Use file search tools):
- Identify existing security frameworks and libraries in use
- Look for established secure coding patterns in the codebase
- Examine existing sanitization and validation patterns
- Understand the project's security model and threat model

Phase 2 - Comparative Analysis:
- Compare new code changes against existing security patterns
- Identify deviations from established secure practices
- Look for inconsistent security implementations
- Flag code that introduces new attack surfaces

Phase 3 - Vulnerability Assessment:
- Examine each modified file for security implications
- Trace data flow from user inputs to sensitive operations
- Look for privilege boundaries being crossed unsafely
- Identify injection points and unsafe deserialization

REQUIRED OUTPUT FORMAT:

You MUST output your findings in markdown. The markdown output should contain the file, line number, severity, category (e.g. `sql_injection` or `xss`), description, exploit scenario, and fix recommendation.

For example:

# Vuln 1: XSS: `foo.py:42`

* Severity: High
* Description: User input from `username` parameter is directly interpolated into HTML without escaping, allowing reflected XSS attacks
* Exploit Scenario: Attacker crafts URL like /bar?q=<script>alert(document.cookie)</script> to execute JavaScript in victim's browser, enabling session hijacking or data theft
* Recommendation: Use Flask's escape() function or Jinja2 templates with auto-escaping enabled for all user inputs rendered in HTML

SEVERITY GUIDELINES:
- **HIGH**: Directly exploitable vulnerabilities leading to RCE, data breach, or authentication bypass
- **MEDIUM**: Vulnerabilities requiring specific conditions but with significant impact
- **LOW**: Defense-in-depth issues or lower-impact vulnerabilities

CONFIDENCE SCORING:
- 0.9-1.0: Certain exploit path identified, tested if possible
- 0.8-0.9: Clear vulnerability pattern with known exploitation methods
- 0.7-0.8: Suspicious pattern requiring specific conditions to exploit
- Below 0.7: Don't report (too speculative)

FINAL REMINDER:
Focus on HIGH and MEDIUM findings only. Better to miss some theoretical issues than flood the report with false positives. Each finding should be something a security engineer would confidently raise in a PR review.

FALSE POSITIVE FILTERING:

> You do not need to run commands to reproduce the vulnerability, just read the code to determine if it is a real vulnerability. Do not use the bash tool or write to any files.
>
> HARD EXCLUSIONS - Automatically exclude findings matching these patterns:
> 1. Denial of Service (DOS) vulnerabilities or resource exhaustion attacks.
> 2. Secrets or credentials stored on disk if they are otherwise secured.
> 3. Rate limiting concerns or service overload scenarios.
> 4. Memory consumption or CPU exhaustion issues.
> 5. Lack of input validation on non-security-critical fields without proven security impact.
> 6. Input sanitization concerns for GitHub Action workflows unless they are clearly triggerable via untrusted input.
> 7. A lack of hardening measures. Code is not expected to implement all security best practices, only flag concrete vulnerabilities.
> 8. Race conditions or timing attacks that are theoretical rather than practical issues. Only report a race condition if it is concretely problematic.
> 9. Vulnerabilities related to outdated third-party libraries. These are managed separately and should not be reported here.
> 10. Memory safety issues such as buffer overflows or use-after-free-vulnerabilities are impossible in rust. Do not report memory safety issues in rust or any other memory safe languages.
> 11. Files that are only unit tests or only used as part of running tests.
> 12. Log spoofing concerns. Outputting un-sanitized user input to logs is not a vulnerability.
> 13. SSRF vulnerabilities that only control the path. SSRF is only a concern if it can control the host or protocol.
> 14. Including user-controlled content in AI system prompts is not a vulnerability.
> 15. Regex injection. Injecting untrusted content into a regex is not a vulnerability.
> 16. Regex DOS concerns.
> 16. Insecure documentation. Do not report any findings in documentation files such as markdown files.
> 17. A lack of audit logs is not a vulnerability.
>
> PRECEDENTS -
> 1. Logging high value secrets in plaintext is a vulnerability. Logging URLs is assumed to be safe.
> 2. UUIDs can be assumed to be unguessable and do not need to be validated.
> 3. Environment variables and CLI flags are trusted values. Attackers are generally not able to modify them in a secure environment. Any attack that relies on controlling an environment variable is invalid.
> 4. Resource management issues such as memory or file descriptor leaks are not valid.
> 5. Subtle or low impact web vulnerabilities such as tabnabbing, XS-Leaks, prototype pollution, and open redirects should not be reported unless they are extremely high confidence.
> 6. React and Angular are generally secure against XSS. These frameworks do not need to sanitize or escape user input unless it is using dangerouslySetInnerHTML, bypassSecurityTrustHtml, or similar methods. Do not report XSS vulnerabilities in React or Angular components or tsx files unless they are using unsafe methods.
> 7. Most vulnerabilities in github action workflows are not exploitable in practice. Before validating a github action workflow vulnerability ensure it is concrete and has a very specific attack path.
> 8. A lack of permission checking or authentication in client-side JS/TS code is not a vulnerability. Client-side code is not trusted and does not need to implement these checks, they are handled on the server-side. The same applies to all flows that send untrusted data to the backend, the backend is responsible for validating and sanitizing all inputs.
> 9. Only include MEDIUM findings if they are obvious and concrete issues.
> 10. Most vulnerabilities in ipython notebooks (*.ipynb files) are not exploitable in practice. Before validating a notebook vulnerability ensure it is concrete and has a very specific attack path where untrusted input can trigger the vulnerability.
> 11. Logging non-PII data is not a vulnerability even if the data may be sensitive. Only report logging vulnerabilities if they expose sensitive information such as secrets, passwords, or personally identifiable information (PII).
> 12. Command injection vulnerabilities in shell scripts are generally not exploitable in practice since shell scripts generally do not run with untrusted user input. Only report command injection vulnerabilities in shell scripts if they are concrete and have a very specific attack path for untrusted input.
>
> SIGNAL QUALITY CRITERIA - For remaining findings, assess:
> 1. Is there a concrete, exploitable vulnerability with a clear attack path?
> 2. Does this represent a real security risk vs theoretical best practice?
> 3. Are there specific code locations and reproduction steps?
> 4. Would this finding be actionable for a security team?
>
> For each finding, assign a confidence score from 1-10:
> - 1-3: Low confidence, likely false positive or noise
> - 4-6: Medium confidence, needs investigation
> - 7-10: High confidence, likely true vulnerability

START ANALYSIS:

Begin your analysis now. Do this in 3 steps:

1. Use a sub-task to identify vulnerabilities. Use the repository exploration tools to understand the codebase context, then analyze the PR changes for security implications. In the prompt for this sub-task, include all of the above.
2. Then for each vulnerability identified by the above sub-task, create a new sub-task to filter out false-positives. Launch these sub-tasks as parallel sub-tasks. In the prompt for these sub-tasks, include everything in the "FALSE POSITIVE FILTERING" instructions.
3. Filter out any vulnerabilities where the sub-task reported a confidence less than 8.

Your final reply must contain the markdown report and nothing else.

# Security Review — reward_weights config-as-data (NFR-VA-010)

**Scope:** `supabase/migrations/20260702150000_autopilot_reward_weights.sql` · `supabase/functions/autopilot-analyze/index.ts` (docs excluded per rules). Analysis performed inline (sub-agent quota exhausted this session); methodology and false-positive filtering applied as specified.

## Findings: NONE (no HIGH or MEDIUM confidence ≥8 vulnerabilities)

Verified attack surfaces, each with the reason it does not qualify:

* **New user-controlled input path (`autopilot_plans.reward_weights` jsonb → service-role engine)** — the column is written under existing RLS-own policies (user can only write their own plan row) and read back tenant-scoped: `.eq("id", planId).eq("user_id", userId)` where `userId` derives from the trusted `autopilot_cycles` row, never the body — the fn's existing anti-IDOR contract is preserved. The value is consumed exclusively as numeric ranking weights after hard validation (`typeof number` + `Number.isFinite` + `>= 0` + `sum > 0`; `ewma_m` int `1..12`; `ewma_decay ∈ (0,1)`); any off-shape input falls back to engine defaults. No SQL string interpolation (supabase-js parameterized), no eval/template/HTML sink, no prototype-merge (specific-key reads only). Cross-tenant impact: none — weights only affect the ranking of the same tenant's own policy.
* **Privilege/authorization** — no new grants, no SECURITY DEFINER, no RLS change; the migration adds one nullable column with a container-type CHECK. Auth gate of the edge fn (service-role Bearer, 403 otherwise) untouched.
* **Billing integrity** — `ANALYZE_COST` fixed at 2 and unchanged; the prepaid path still charges 0; weights cannot alter any deduct amount. `ewma_m` cap (12) bounds the window query.
* **Data exposure** — `reward_vector.weights`/`weights_source` written to the tenant's own `optimization_policy` row; no PII/secrets, no new logging of sensitive values.
* **Degenerate self-config edge case (informational, not a vulnerability):** four huge finite weights (e.g. `1e308` each) can sum to `Infinity`, normalizing all weights to `0` and making the ranking arbitrary — strictly tenant-local, no crash, no cross-tenant or billing effect. Optional hardening: cap individual weights (e.g. `<= 1e6`).

**Verdict: SAFE** — no findings at confidence ≥8. Migration cleared for apply per the project rule (review before production apply).
